# Agents · C · part 7

959 entries, codex-consultant to completeness-checker. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **codex-consultant**: Use this agent when the user asks for code review, plan review, solution debates, architecture decisions, or wants a second opinion from an external perspective. This agent should be used proactively whenever significant code is written, implementation plans are proposed, or when weighing multiple approaches to a problem. Examples: &lt;example&gt; Context: User asks for a code review of recently written code. user: "Can you review this authentication middleware I just wrote?" assistant: "I'll use the codex-consultant agent to get an external expert review of your authentication middleware." &lt;commen… ([rube-de/cc-skills](https://github.com/rube-de/cc-skills/blob/HEAD/plugins/council/agents/codex-consultant.md) · ★10 · 3 copies)
- **codex-consultant**: Use this agent when the user asks to consult with Codex for code analysis, explanation, or insights. This agent is particularly useful when you need deep code understanding, architectural analysis, or when the user explicitly mentions wanting to 'consult with codex' or 'ask codex about' specific files or code patterns. ([jleechanorg/jleechan-skills](https://github.com/jleechanorg/jleechan-skills/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/codex-consultant.md) · ★4)
- **codex-critic**: Adversarial reviewer — GPT (Codex) via mcp__codex__codex. Read-only sandbox. Used for adversarial code/plan review in multi-LLM review tasks. Optional; requires ChatGPT subscription + Codex MCP. ([moongci38-oss/multi-llm-review](https://github.com/moongci38-oss/multi-llm-review/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-critic.md) · ★1)
- **codex-cross-reviewer**: Cross-review orchestrator using Codex CLI as 2nd reviewer. Runs structured finding exchange with consensus loop (max 5 rounds), then user escalation. ([babyworm/rtl-agent-team](https://github.com/babyworm/rtl-agent-team/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-cross-reviewer.md) · ★52)
- **codex-debugger**: Root-cause a failing test, crash, stack trace, or misbehaving feature by delegating the investigation to OpenAI Codex (GPT-5) via the local Codex CLI, then verifying its diagnosis against the real code. Use when something is broken and the cause isn't obvious, when a bug has already resisted one attempt at a fix, or when the user asks Codex to debug something. Runs read-only — it diagnoses and proposes a patch, it does not apply one. ([Sateezg/codex-bridge](https://github.com/Sateezg/codex-bridge/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-debugger.md) · ★248)
- **codex-debugger**: Error analysis and complex problem-solving specialist powered by Codex CLI. Use proactively when encountering errors, test failures, build failures, or unexpected behavior. Also use for complex debugging that requires deep reasoning. Automatically suggested by hooks when errors are detected. ([DeL-TaiseiOzaki/claude-code-orchestra](https://github.com/DeL-TaiseiOzaki/claude-code-orchestra/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/codex-debugger.md) · ★194 · 7 copies)
- **codex-delegate**: Delegate code review, debugging, PRD writing, and implementation tasks to OpenAI Codex. Best for focused code analysis, bug finding, and technical writing. ([agentic-box/owlex](https://github.com/agentic-box/owlex/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-delegate.md) · ★139)
- **codex-delegate**: Delegate a well-specified implementation, refactor, migration, or bulk-mechanical coding task to the configured Codex CLI model and return ONLY a compact summary. Keeps Codex's large transcript out of the orchestrator's context. Use for multi-step tasks; not for tiny one-shot edits. ([congmnguyen/claude-code-wsl2-setup](https://github.com/congmnguyen/claude-code-wsl2-setup/blob/HEAD/archive/agents/codex-delegate.md) · ★50)
- **codex-delegator**: Delegate complex coding tasks to Codex with explicit configuration checks and user-confirmed bootstrap steps. ([mustafaakben/codex-agent-plugin](https://github.com/mustafaakben/codex-agent-plugin/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-delegator.md) · ★6)
- **codex-dependency-shortcut**: When the user types exactly `dependency` as the entire task message, after trimming surrounding whitespace, treat it as a shortcut for safe dependency maintenance. This is a Codex chat shortcut, not an app feature, script, automation, or CI workflow. ([BigSimmo/Database](https://github.com/BigSimmo/Database/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/codex-dependency-shortcut.md))
- **codex-design-reviewer**: Independent design reviewer powered by Codex CLI. - **codex-desktop-worktree-setup**: - The Windows Codex Desktop environment setup command is `node scripts/setup-codex-worktree.mjs`. It must work before `node_modules` exists, validate Node 24/npm 11, reuse only a complete byte-identical local installation, and otherwise run the locked npm install. - Never configure Windows Desktop worktrees to run `bash scripts/setup-codex-cloud.sh`. That script is Linux/Cloud-only; Windows launch ([BigSimmo/Database](https://github.com/BigSimmo/Database/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/codex-desktop-worktree-setup.md))

- **codex-dispatcher**: Dispatches an already-prepared batch of codex-swarm subtask prompt files to parallel `codex exec` sessions, waits for all of them, writes the run log, and returns only a short summary plus the log path. Use it from the codex-swarm skill after decomposition — pass it the task-file paths, the mode flag (--auto, --readonly or --yolo), and the project root. It must never forward raw Codex output. ([Vallykrie/swarmGPT](https://github.com/Vallykrie/swarmGPT/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-dispatcher.md) · ★2)
- **codex-doc-updater**: Thin MCP wrapper that dispatches documentation updates to Codex MCP ([kenkenmain/ken-cc-plugins](https://github.com/kenkenmain/ken-cc-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/subagents/agents/codex-doc-updater.md) · ★1)
- **codex-doer**: Codex worker that drafts patches without write tools. ([SpillwaveSolutions/okf-agent-graph](https://github.com/SpillwaveSolutions/okf-agent-graph/blob/HEAD/tests/fixtures/plugin-repo/hosts/codex-open/codex/agents/codex-doer.md) · ★1)
- **codex-driver**: Delegate one read-only task to Codex headless (codex exec). Use when an auditor needs Codex (e.g. a semantic/architectural audit). The caller passes the audit prompt; this agent runs exactly one read-only Codex invocation and returns its stdout verbatim. It is audit-only — it never writes files — and does not inspect the repository, choose a model, or improvise. ([WeZZard/amplify](https://github.com/WeZZard/amplify/blob/HEAD/agents/codex-driver.md) · ★7)

- **codex-implementer**: Default (routine) implementation lane running GPT-5.6 Luna via the OpenAI Codex CLI (`codex exec`), at whatever reasoning effort the architect names in the spec. Route routine, well-specified work here — the spec fully determines the outcome and Codex does the typing at a fraction of the architect's token cost, from a different model family than the session. Receives the standard six-part spec; drives codex to write the code; returns a structured report with verification evidence. Use for mechanical work across many files (renames, import migrations, adding a pattern everywhere), bulk generation (scaffolding, fixtures, repetitive tests), or a self-contained module port.
